1Password CLI brings 1Password to your terminal. Learn how to install the CLI, then integrate it with your 1Password app and sign in with Touch ID, Windows Hello, or another system authentication option.

## [New to 1Password developer tools?](https://www.1password.dev/get-started/developer-quickstart)

Follow the developer quickstart to set up the CLI, secure your secrets, and add biometric SSH signing in one guide.

## Step 1: Install 1Password CLI

Requirements

Supported shells: Bash, Zsh, sh, fish

\*Required to integrate 1Password CLI with the 1Password app.

- [1Password subscription](https://1password.com/pricing/password-manager)
- [1Password for Windows](https://1password.com/downloads/windows)

Supported shells: PowerShell

- [1Password subscription](https://1password.com/pricing/password-manager)
- [1Password for Linux](https://1password.com/downloads/linux) \*
- [PolKit](https://github.com/polkit-org/polkit) \*
- A PolKit authentication agent running\*

Supported shells: Bash, Zsh, sh, fish

\*Required to integrate 1Password CLI with the 1Password app.

- homebrew
- Manual

1. To install 1Password CLI with [homebrew](https://brew.sh/):
	```shellscript
	brew install 1password-cli
	```
2. Check that 1Password CLI installed successfully:
	```shellscript
	op --version
	```
The [1Password Homebrew cask](https://formulae.brew.sh/cask/1password-cli) is maintained by both Homebrew and 1Password developers in the open-source [Homebrew Cask repository.](https://github.com/Homebrew/homebrew-cask) 1Password CLI downloads from our CDN on agilebits.net/.com domains.

To manually install 1Password CLI on macOS:

1. Download [the latest release of 1Password CLI](https://app-updates.agilebits.com/product_history/CLI2).  
	Learn how to [verify its authenticity](https://www.1password.dev/cli/verify).
2. - **Package file**: Open `op.pkg` and install 1Password CLI in the default location (`usr/local/bin`).
		- **ZIP file**: Open `op.zip` and unzip the file, then move `op` to `usr/local/bin`.
3. Check that 1Password CLI was installed successfully:
	```shellscript
	op --version
	```

- winget
- Manual

1. To install 1Password CLI with winget:
	```powershell
	winget install 1password-cli
	```
2. Check that 1Password CLI installed successfully:
	```powershell
	op --version
	```

To manually install 1Password CLI on Windows:

1. Download [the latest release of 1Password CLI](https://app-updates.agilebits.com/product_history/CLI2) and extract `op.exe`.  
	Learn how to [verify its authenticity](https://www.1password.dev/cli/verify).
2. Open PowerShell **as an administrator**.
3. Create a folder to move `op.exe` into. For example, `C:\Program Files\1Password CLI`.
	```powershell
	mkdir "C:\Program Files\1Password CLI"
	```
4. Move the `op.exe` file to the new folder.
	```powershell
	mv ".\op.exe" "C:\Program Files\1Password CLI"
	```
5. Add the folder containing the op.exe file to your PATH.
	**Windows 10 and later**
	1. Search for **Advanced System Settings** in the Start menu.
	2. Select **Environment Variables**.
	3. In the System Variables section, select the **PATH** environment variable and select **Edit**.
	4. In the prompt, select **New** and add the directory where `op.exe` is located.
	5. Sign out and back in to Windows for the change to take effect.
6. Check that 1Password CLI installed successfully:
	```shellscript
	op --version
	```

If you’d rather install 1Password CLI with a single block of commands, run the following in PowerShell as administrator:

```powershell
$arch = (Get-CimInstance Win32_OperatingSystem).OSArchitecture
switch ($arch) {
    '64-bit' { $opArch = 'amd64'; break }
    '32-bit' { $opArch = '386'; break }
    Default { Write-Error "Sorry, your operating system architecture '$arch' is unsupported" -ErrorAction Stop }
}
$installDir = Join-Path -Path $env:ProgramFiles -ChildPath '1Password CLI'
Invoke-WebRequest -Uri "https://cache.agilebits.com/dist/1P/op2/pkg/v2.39.0/op_windows_$($opArch)_v2.39.0.zip" -OutFile op.zip
Expand-Archive -Path op.zip -DestinationPath $installDir -Force
$envMachinePath = [System.Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('PATH','machine')
if ($envMachinePath -split ';' -notcontains $installDir){
    [Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable('PATH', "$envMachinePath;$installDir", 'Machine')
}
Remove-Item -Path op.zip
```

If your Windows operating system uses a language other than English, you’ll need to manually set `$arch` in the first line. To do this, replace `$arch = (Get-CimInstance Win32_OperatingSystem).OSArchitecture` with `$arch = “64-bit”` or `$arch = “32-bit”`.

- APT
- YUM
- Alpine
- NixOS
- Manual

To install 1Password CLI using APT on Debian- and Ubuntu-based distributions:

1. Run the following command:
	```shellscript
	curl -sS https://downloads.1password.com/linux/keys/1password.asc | \
	  sudo gpg --dearmor --output /usr/share/keyrings/1password-archive-keyring.gpg && \
	  echo "deb [arch=$(dpkg --print-architecture) signed-by=/usr/share/keyrings/1password-archive-keyring.gpg] https://downloads.1password.com/linux/debian/$(dpkg --print-architecture) stable main" | \
	  sudo tee /etc/apt/sources.list.d/1password.list && \
	  sudo mkdir -p /etc/debsig/policies/AC2D62742012EA22/ && \
	  curl -sS https://downloads.1password.com/linux/debian/debsig/1password.pol | \
	  sudo tee /etc/debsig/policies/AC2D62742012EA22/1password.pol && \
	  sudo mkdir -p /usr/share/debsig/keyrings/AC2D62742012EA22 && \
	  curl -sS https://downloads.1password.com/linux/keys/1password.asc | \
	  sudo gpg --dearmor --output /usr/share/debsig/keyrings/AC2D62742012EA22/debsig.gpg && \
	  sudo apt update && sudo apt install 1password-cli
	```
	See a step-by-step version of the script
	1. Add the key for the 1Password `apt` repository:
		```shellscript
		curl -sS https://downloads.1password.com/linux/keys/1password.asc | \
		  sudo gpg --dearmor --output /usr/share/keyrings/1password-archive-keyring.gpg
		```
	2. Add the 1Password `apt` repository:
		```shellscript
		echo "deb [arch=$(dpkg --print-architecture) signed-by=/usr/share/keyrings/1password-archive-keyring.gpg] https://downloads.1password.com/linux/debian/$(dpkg --print-architecture) stable main" |
		  sudo tee /etc/apt/sources.list.d/1password.list
		```
	3. Add the debsig-verify policy:
		```shellscript
		sudo mkdir -p /etc/debsig/policies/AC2D62742012EA22/
		  curl -sS https://downloads.1password.com/linux/debian/debsig/1password.pol | \
		  sudo tee /etc/debsig/policies/AC2D62742012EA22/1password.pol && \
		  sudo mkdir -p /usr/share/debsig/keyrings/AC2D62742012EA22 && \
		  curl -sS https://downloads.1password.com/linux/keys/1password.asc | \
		  sudo gpg --dearmor --output /usr/share/debsig/keyrings/AC2D62742012EA22/debsig.gpg
		```
	4. Install 1Password CLI:
		```shellscript
		sudo apt update && sudo apt install 1password-cli
		```
2. Check that 1Password CLI installed successfully:
	```shellscript
	op --version
	```

Alternatively, download the latest 1Password CLI `.deb` package directly from one of the following links:

- [amd64](https://downloads.1password.com/linux/debian/amd64/stable/1password-cli-amd64-latest.deb)
- [386](https://downloads.1password.com/linux/debian/386/stable/1password-cli-386-latest.deb)
- [arm](https://downloads.1password.com/linux/debian/arm/stable/1password-cli-arm-latest.deb)
- [arm64](https://downloads.1password.com/linux/debian/arm64/stable/1password-cli-arm64-latest.deb)

To install 1Password CLI using YUM on RPM-based distributions:

1. Run the following commands:
	```shellscript
	sudo rpm --import https://downloads.1password.com/linux/keys/1password.asc
	sudo sh -c 'echo -e "[1password]\nname=1Password Stable Channel\nbaseurl=https://downloads.1password.com/linux/rpm/stable/\$basearch\nenabled=1\ngpgcheck=1\nrepo_gpgcheck=1\ngpgkey=\"https://downloads.1password.com/linux/keys/1password.asc\"" > /etc/yum.repos.d/1password.repo'
	sudo dnf check-update -y 1password-cli && sudo dnf install 1password-cli
	```
	The above script is comprised of the following steps
	1. Import the public key:
		```shellscript
		sudo rpm --import https://downloads.1password.com/linux/keys/1password.asc
		```
	2. Configure the repository information:
		```shellscript
		sudo sh -c 'echo -e "[1password]\nname=1Password Stable Channel\nbaseurl=https://downloads.1password.com/linux/rpm/stable/\$basearch\nenabled=1\ngpgcheck=1\nrepo_gpgcheck=1\ngpgkey=\"https://downloads.1password.com/linux/keys/1password.asc\"" > /etc/yum.repos.d/1password.repo'
		```
	3. Install 1Password CLI:
		```shellscript
		sudo dnf check-update -y 1password-cli && sudo dnf install 1password-cli
		```
2. Check that 1Password CLI installed successfully:
	```shellscript
	op --version
	```

Alternatively, download the latest 1Password CLI `.rpm` package directly from one of the following links:

- [x86\_64](https://downloads.1password.com/linux/rpm/stable/x86_64/1password-cli-latest.x86_64.rpm)
- [i386](https://downloads.1password.com/linux/rpm/stable/i386/1password-cli-latest.i386.rpm)
- [aarch64](https://downloads.1password.com/linux/rpm/stable/aarch64/1password-cli-latest.aarch64.rpm)
- [armv7l](https://downloads.1password.com/linux/rpm/stable/armv7l/1password-cli-latest.armv7l.rpm)

To install 1Password CLI on Alpine x86\_64 distributions:

1. Run the following commands:
	```shellscript
	echo https://downloads.1password.com/linux/alpinelinux/stable/ >> /etc/apk/repositories
	wget https://downloads.1password.com/linux/keys/alpinelinux/support@1password.com-61ddfc31.rsa.pub -P /etc/apk/keys
	apk update && apk add 1password-cli
	```
	The above script is comprised of the following steps
	1. Add Password CLI to your list of repositories:
		```shellscript
		echo https://downloads.1password.com/linux/alpinelinux/stable/ >> /etc/apk/repositories
		```
	2. Add the public key to validate the APK to your keys directory:
		```shellscript
		wget https://downloads.1password.com/linux/keys/alpinelinux/support@1password.com-61ddfc31.rsa.pub -P /etc/apk/keys
		```
	3. Install 1Password CLI:
		```shellscript
		apk update && apk add 1password-cli
		```
2. Check that 1Password CLI installed successfully:
	```shellscript
	op --version
	```

The Nix package is available from the NixOS open source community.

To install 1Password CLI on your NixOS system:

1. Add 1Password to your `/etc/nixos/configuration.nix` file, or `flake.nix` if you’re using a flake. For example, the following snippet includes 1Password CLI and the 1Password app:
	```nix
	# NixOS has built-in modules to enable 1Password
	# along with some pre-packaged configuration to make
	# it work nicely. You can search what options exist
	# in NixOS at https://search.nixos.org/options
	# Enables the 1Password CLI
	programs._1password = { enable = true; };
	# Enables the 1Password desktop app
	programs._1password-gui = {
	enable = true;
	# this makes system auth etc. work properly
	polkitPolicyOwners = [ "<your-linux-username>" ];
	};
	```
2. After you make changes to your configuration file, apply them:
	- If you added 1Password to `/etc.nixos/configuration.nix`, run:
		```shellscript
		sudo nixos-rebuild switch
		```
		- If you added 1Password to `flake.nix`, replace `<flake-directory-path>` with the directory your flake is in and `<output-name>` with the name of the flake output containing your system configuration, then run the command.
		```shellscript
		sudo nixos-rebuild switch --flake <flake-directory-path>.#<output-name>
		```
3. Check that 1Password CLI installed successfully:
	```shellscript
	op --version
	```

Learn more about [using 1Password on NixOS.](https://nixos.wiki/wiki/1Password)

To install 1Password CLI on Linux without a package manager:

```shellscript
ARCH="<choose between 386/amd64/arm/arm64>" && \
wget "https://cache.agilebits.com/dist/1P/op2/pkg/v2.39.0/op_linux_${ARCH}_v2.39.0.zip" -O op.zip && \
unzip -d op op.zip && \
sudo mv op/op /usr/local/bin/ && \
rm -r op.zip op && \
sudo groupadd -f onepassword-cli && \
sudo chgrp onepassword-cli /usr/local/bin/op && \
sudo chmod g+s /usr/local/bin/op
```

Or follow the extended guide

1. Download the [latest release of 1Password CLI](https://app-updates.agilebits.com/product_history/CLI2) and extract it. To verify its authenticity:
	```shellscript
	gpg --keyserver keyserver.ubuntu.com --receive-keys 3FEF9748469ADBE15DA7CA80AC2D62742012EA22
	gpg --verify op.sig op
	```
2. Move `op` to `/usr/local/bin`, or another directory in your `$PATH`.
3. Check that 1Password CLI installed successfully:
	```shellscript
	op --version
	```
4. Create the `onepassword-cli` group if it doesn’t yet exist:
	```shellscript
	sudo groupadd onepassword-cli
	```
5. Set the correct permissions on the `op` binary:
	```shellscript
	sudo chgrp onepassword-cli /usr/local/bin/op && \
	sudo chmod g+s /usr/local/bin/op
	```

</li>
</ol><small>The <a href="https://formulae.brew.sh/cask/1password-cli">1Password Homebrew cask <svg class="forced-colors:forced-color-adjust-none forced-colors:bg-[color:CanvasText]! icon inline bg-primary dark:bg-primary-light" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false"></svg></a> is maintained by both Homebrew and 1Password developers in the open-source <a href="https://github.com/Homebrew/homebrew-cask" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">Homebrew Cask repository. <svg class="forced-colors:forced-color-adjust-none forced-colors:bg-[color:CanvasText]! icon inline bg-primary dark:bg-primary-light" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false"></svg></a> 1Password CLI downloads from our CDN on agilebits.net/.com domains. </small></div><div id="panel-manual-1" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="manual" tabindex="0" class="prose dark:prose-invert overflow-x-auto [&amp;_[data-table-wrapper]]:![--page-padding:0px] [&amp;_[role=&quot;listitem&quot;]]:pl-4 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)_img]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child[data-table-wrapper]]:pt-0! hidden" data-component-part="tab-content"><span data-as="p">To manually install 1Password CLI on macOS:</span><ol>
<li>
<span data-as="p">Download <a href="https://app-updates.agilebits.com/product_history/CLI2" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">the latest release of 1Password CLI</a>.<br> <small>Learn how to <a class="link" href="https://www.1password.dev/cli/verify">verify its authenticity</a>.</small></span>
</li>
<li data-no-bullet="">
<ul>
<li><strong>Package file</strong>: Open <code>op.pkg</code> and install 1Password CLI in the default location (<code>usr/local/bin</code>).</li>
<li><strong>ZIP file</strong>: Open <code>op.zip</code> and unzip the file, then move <code>op</code> to <code>usr/local/bin</code>.</li>
</ul>
</li>

<li>
<span data-as="p">Download <a href="https://app-updates.agilebits.com/product_history/CLI2" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">the latest release of 1Password CLI</a> and extract <code>op.exe</code>.<br> <small>Learn how to <a class="link" href="https://www.1password.dev/cli/verify">verify its authenticity</a>.</small></span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p">Open PowerShell <strong>as an administrator</strong>.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p">Create a folder to move <code>op.exe</code> into. For example, <code>C:\Program Files\1Password CLI</code>.</span>

[--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] py-3.5 px-4 overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport"><div role="presentation" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="min-w-full! h-full children:my-0! children:shadow-none! children:bg-transparent!"><div class="font-mono whitespace-pre leading-6"><pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light-default dark-plus" language="powershell"><code language="powershell" numberoflines="14"><span class="line"><span>$arch</span><span> =</span><span> (</span><span>Get-CimInstance</span><span> Win32_OperatingSystem).OSArchitecture</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>switch</span><span> (</span><span>$arch</span><span>) {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>    '64-bit'</span><span> { </span><span>$opArch</span><span> =</span><span> 'amd64'</span><span>; </span><span>break</span><span> }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>    '32-bit'</span><span> { </span><span>$opArch</span><span> =</span><span> '386'</span><span>; </span><span>break</span><span> }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>    Default</span><span> { </span><span>Write-Error</span><span> "Sorry, your operating system architecture '</span><span>$arch</span><span>' is unsupported"</span><span> -</span><span>ErrorAction Stop }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>}</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>$installDir</span><span> =</span><span> Join-Path</span><span> -</span><span>Path </span><span>$</span><span>env:</span><span>ProgramFiles</span><span> -</span><span>ChildPath </span><span>'1Password CLI'</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Invoke-WebRequest</span><span> -</span><span>Uri </span><span>"https://cache.agilebits.com/dist/1P/op2/pkg/v2.39.0/op_windows_</span><span>$(</span><span>$opArch</span><span>)</span><span>_v2.39.0.zip"</span><span> -</span><span>OutFile op.zip</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Expand-Archive</span><span> -</span><span>Path op.zip </span><span>-</span><span>DestinationPath </span><span>$installDir</span><span> -</span><span>Force</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>$envMachinePath</span><span> =</span><span> [</span><span>System.Environment</span><span>]::GetEnvironmentVariable(</span><span>'PATH'</span><span>,</span><span>'machine'</span><span>)</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>if</span><span> (</span><span>$envMachinePath</span><span> -split</span><span> ';'</span><span> -notcontains</span><span> $installDir</span><span>){</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>    [</span><span>Environment</span><span>]::SetEnvironmentVariable(</span><span>'PATH'</span><span>,</span><span> "</span><span>$envMachinePath</span><span>;</span><span>$installDir</span><span>"</span><span>,</span><span> 'Machine'</span><span>)</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>}</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Remove-Item</span><span> -</span><span>Path op.zip</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true"></div></div><small><span data-as="p">If your Windows operating system uses a language other than English, you’ll need to manually set <code>$arch</code> in the first line. To do this, replace <code>$arch = (Get-CimInstance Win32_OperatingSystem).OSArchitecture</code> with <code>$arch = “64-bit”</code> or <code>$arch = “32-bit”</code>.</span></small></div></div></div></div><div id="panel-linux-2-2" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="linux-2" tabindex="0" class="prose dark:prose-invert overflow-x-auto <span class="line"><span>sudo</span><span> dnf</span><span> check-update</span><span> -y</span><span> 1password-cli</span><span> &amp;&amp; </span><span>sudo</span><span> dnf</span><span> install</span><span> 1password-cli</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true"></div></div>

language="nix"><code language="nix" numberoflines="14"><span class="line"><span># NixOS has built-in modules to enable 1Password</span></span>
<span class="line"><span># along with some pre-packaged configuration to make</span></span>
<span class="line"><span># it work nicely. You can search what options exist</span></span>
<span class="line"><span># in NixOS at https://search.nixos.org/options</span></span>
<span class="line"></span>
<span class="line"><span># Enables the 1Password CLI</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>programs</span><span>.</span><span>_1password</span><span> =</span><span> { </span><span>enable</span><span> =</span><span> true</span><span>; }</span><span>;</span></span>
<span class="line"></span>
<span class="line"><span># Enables the 1Password desktop app</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>programs</span><span>.</span><span>_1password-gui</span><span> =</span><span> {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>enable</span><span> =</span><span> true</span><span>;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span># this makes system auth etc. work properly</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>polkitPolicyOwners</span><span> =</span><span> [ </span><span>"&lt;your-linux-username&gt;"</span><span> ];</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>}</span><span>;</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true"></div></div>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p">After you make changes to your configuration file, apply them:</span>
<ul>

<span class="line"><span>wget</span><span> "https://cache.agilebits.com/dist/1P/op2/pkg/v2.39.0/op_linux_${</span><span>ARCH</span><span>}_v2.39.0.zip"</span><span> -O</span><span> op.zip</span><span> &amp;&amp; </span><span>\</span></span>

<span class="line"><span>rm</span><span> -r</span><span> op.zip</span><span> op</span><span> &amp;&amp; </span><span>\</span></span>

<span class="line"><span>sudo</span><span> chgrp</span><span> onepassword-cli</span><span> /usr/local/bin/op</span><span> &amp;&amp; </span><span>\</span></span>

<li>Open and unlock the <a href="https://1password.com/downloads/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">1Password app</a>.</li>
<li>Select your account or collection at the top of the sidebar.</li>
<li>Navigate to <strong>Settings</strong> &gt; <strong><a href="onepassword://settings/developers" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">Developer</a></strong>.</li>
<li>Select <strong>Integrate with 1Password CLI</strong>.</li>
<li>If you want to authenticate 1Password CLI with your fingerprint, turn on <strong><a href="https://support.1password.com/touch-id-mac/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">Touch ID</a></strong> in the app.</li>

[&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-green-800 dark:text-green-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">You can also authenticate 1Password CLI using a <a class="link" href="https://www.1password.dev/service-accounts/get-started">service account token</a> scoped to specific vaults or Environments.
